## 3. Per floor (07 §8.2)

*Every arrival on a floor by every recipe of that party size, all death rules pooled; "arrivals" counts the delves that stood on the floor. The band is the floor's threat chip for the party on arrival (D-092). Gold is what was found on the floor; its target is `07` §2.3's.*

## 12. Reading the report

- Targets are data: tuning.json → `runner.targets` and each recipe's `clearTarget` (D-084's for the Cellars; the Crypts' and the Vault's carried over, Proposed). Only Normal without Pacts is judged; Hard, Dread and Pacts are reported.
- A **fight** is 3–6 rounds for ordinary packs (03 §3.6), elites 6–8, bosses 10–15, hunters 6–8.
- Gold per cleared delve should land near **R** at the delve's band (03 §8.1; 0.7–1.3 R), with the full repair bill 15–35% of R; a fully used weapon should lose 25–35% per delve (08 §9.4); the party should be surprised in 10–30% of fights (03 §4.4).
- **Deaths** are falls past the revive window: under Mortal and Nightmare each is a dead hero (D-088); they are reported until James sets targets.
- **Stuck** means the scripted player found no legal way on (sim/src/policies.ts never wanders); with **capped** (the command cap) it should never happen.
- A hook the sim has no joint for logs `hookStub` (sim/src/skills/custom/README.md) and §10 lists the ones that fired.

### 13.1 What changed since round 2 (`2026-10-28-gravehollow-round2.md`)

- **A fight's gold undoes the party-size count rule** (Proposed — a formula change, `sim/src/loot.ts` `fightGold`; round 2 §13.3.4 proposed it). The table's gold range is a 3-hero pack's; the count rule had scaled the pack (×0.6 / 0.8 / 1.0 / 1.6 for 1–4 heroes) and its gold with it, so a four's fight paid 1.6× a trio's and a solo's 0.6×. Now the Fodder and Elites the count rule scaled pay as the 3-hero pack, while a Special, a Monster, a hunter or a boss — which the rule never adds or removes — pays as before, and a preset's extra monsters (Hard's count knob) still pay. Round 2's runner proposal divided every monster by 3 × the count scale; that would have paid a solo 1.7× for every boss and Special, so the correction is limited to the roles the rule scales. *Reading:* `06` §8.5 "a delve … lands on R" — one R for every party, prices written in R.
- **Field skills grow by use** (a fix against `03` §5.2's "each successful use … the same rank curve"; found with pass B). The delve added a disarm's, a First Aid revive's and a secret's XP to the hero's field skill but no code turned XP into rank, so every field skill stayed at its starting rank. Every grant now goes through `progress.ts` `gainFieldSkillXp` (rank N at 100 × 1.5^(N−1); a starting rank counts from its own threshold). **Cartography trains by mapping** (Proposed): each room of a floor entered for the first time pays the hero whose rank draws the map `xp.skill.xpPerUse` × D, within the gate and the per-delve use budget (`trainCartography`; `09` §5).
- **Champion HP by zone** (Proposed — a formula change, `sim/src/explore.ts` `championHpPercent`, data in `tuning.json` → `dungeon.championHpPercentByZone`): a champion's HP (the elite affix's ×3, D-084) × a zone's percent — the Cellars 133% (×4), the Crypts and the Vault 100%.
- **The runner says who deals a boss fight's damage** (§4, new table) and **judges a boss fight on its rounds to kill** (§5: the fights the party won; a lost boss fight runs on while the heroes fall one by one, which is not the length `03` §3.6 describes — every boss fight's mean stays in the table). `--set` reaches loot tables (`loot-table:<id>.<path>=<value>`), and `tools/balance/summary.ts` prints boss, ordinary and elite rounds and gold against R for each what-if.
- **The Dread reference party** (round 2 §13.4): the classic four and the crypt-breakers at L16 with C gear — the F8 ready levels plus two — on all four stretches, played on Dread (`runner.recipes` set `dread`; `runner.plans.gravehollow` part `dread`), reported until James sets Dread targets.
- **Systems polish with no balance effect on the runner's parties** (they wear no relics and the runner gears them itself): each class's default AI rule names the relics that suit it (`tools/content/gen-class-*.ts`, lint rule 80); auto-equip weighs set bonuses (`sim/src/armory.ts`); the hero card's Detection counts the loadout passives (`hero.ts` `heroCardStats`, the number the delve reads).

### 13.3 Still off, and the smallest fix

1. **Boss rounds where the targets do not allow 10–15** (33 LOW, 13 HIGH of 102 lines). *Short:* the parties that out-level a boss — the levelled and ready recipes and the ground solos — kill it in 7.7–10 rounds (the Crypts' ground solo, eight levels over the Warden, in 7.7; the ready pairs and crypt-breakers in 8.5–9.6). *Long:* the solos that do beat Naesa (16.5) and the fresh solo who beats Vexa (18.8, 9 wins in 100) fight long, and so does Vexa's four (16.5): the four's boss HP is 1.85 because at 1.7 the Crypts' and F7's fours cleared 78–80% under Sheltered. Each boss's HP moves every party size together, so lengthening the ready parties' fights (HP +10%) costs the edge lines below — the Crypts' ready pair (59%), the throne's ground solo (44%) and the Crypts' arriving solo (10%). *Smallest fix:* none by number this pass; for James — the 10–15 rounds is the length for the parties a boss is meant for.
2. **Elite fight length** (16 OK of 82). The Cellars' short end is better (the fresh four 6.1, the pair 5.9 with a champion at ×4) but the levelled parties still finish in 3.7–4.5, and the Vault's small parties' elites still run 12–16 rounds: their length is not HP — at ×2 the Vault pair's elites still ran 9–10 and the ground solo's 12, and switching off the three Vault affixes one by one (Regenerating, Vampiric, Shielded with Mirrored and Warded, Leech with Nullifier) left them at 13 rounds or more — but the Vault champions' Defense against a two-hero or one-hero party's damage (the Vault Sentinel). *Smallest fix (Proposed, for checkpoint 2):* champion Defense by zone, or a lower Defense for the Vault Sentinel as a champion; or accept long Vault elites for small parties.
3. **The edges.** Three lines sit within a point or two of their band: the Cellars' fresh solo (9%, 0–10), the Crypts' arriving solo (10%, 0–10) and the Crypts' four under Sheltered (74%, 40–75). 100 seeds a line carry about ±9 points (two standard errors), so a future run can flip them; the solos' boss damage cannot rise without sinking the throne's ground solo (44%: at 0.4 it fell to 34%).
4. **Unchanged from round 1 and 2** (reported in §5–§7): fight and hunter rounds, the ambush rate (most recipes below 10% of fights ambushed), the repair bill (fours above 35% of R) and main-hand wear (below 25% for most starters). The level-13 class spread (1.5×) is pass A's measure, unchanged.

### 13.4 Reported, not tuned

- **Dread.** The Dread reference party (the classic four and the crypt-breakers at L16 with C gear) clears the Cellars 96% / 94%, the Crypts 19% / 21%, F7 8% / 1% and F8 0% / 0%. Dread's knobs (`07` §6.1: enemy HP 150%, damage 145%, count 125%, an extra elite affix, the hunter at 24 ticks, light 60, no revives) take the Crypts' floors from 8–11 levels below the party to a 19–21% clear: the party dies on F4–F5 (it reaches the Warden in a quarter of its delves and beats him 76–81% of the time) and on F7–F8. The main recipes on Dread clear nothing at their own levels (the spot set). *For James:* Dread's targets — and whether its reference party should be stronger (L20, B gear) or Dread gentler.
- **Hard** (the spot set, 40 seeds): the levelled Cellars parties 18% / 25%, the ready Crypts crypt-breakers 35% and pair 10%, F7's ready parties 15% / 10%, F8's 5% / 15%; reported until James sets Hard targets.
- **The dive set:** a fresh four sent into the Crypts clears 43% (round 2: 27%) — the Warden is gentler on a party that dives — and a level-5 four sent into the Vault 1% (0%).

### 13.5 Proposed decisions from Phase 8, pass D (for `13`)

1. **A fight's gold undoes the party-size count rule** (formula; `loot.ts` `fightGold`): the Fodder and Elites the count rule scaled pay as the 3-hero pack the table was written for; Specials, Monsters, hunters and bosses — which the rule never adds or removes — pay as before; a preset's count knob still pays. *Reason:* `06` §8.5 "a delve … lands on R" (one R for every party, prices in R); fours found about 1.4 R. The narrower reading than round 2's proposal (divide everything by 3 × the count scale) keeps a solo's boss purse and Specials at their price.
2. **The boss row retuned** (D-128's numbers): HP ×0.4 / 0.7 / 1.2 / 1.85, damage ×0.35 / 0.6 / 0.65 / 0.75, with the bosses' own Attack raised where their blows carry the fight (Vexa ×1.15 → ×2.8, Naesa ×1.05 → ×3.0; the Warden ×0.75 → ×1.0; the King kept at ×0.9, his blows softer — at ×1.2 the throne's four fell to 39%) and the Warden's and the King's HP ×9, Naesa's ×10. *Reason:* `03` §3.6 "bosses 10–15 with phases": a boss's adds and arena calls deal most of its fight's damage (the Warden's own blows 8%), so a longer fight was deadlier however softly the boss hit; halving the adds' weight let HP lengthen the fights with the clear rates inside.
3. **Champion HP by zone** (formula; `explore.ts` `championHpPercent`, `tuning.json` → `dungeon.championHpPercentByZone`): the Cellars ×4 (133% of the affix's ×3), the Crypts and the Vault ×3. *Reason:* `03` §3.6 "elites 6–8"; the Cellars' elites fought 3–5 rounds. It fixes that end only (§13.3.2).
4. **Field skills grow by use; Cartography trains by mapping** (a fix against `03` §5.2, and a reading of "map"): every field-skill XP grant reaches rank on the skill curve (a starting rank counts from its own threshold); each room of a floor entered for the first time pays the hero whose Cartography draws the map `xp.skill.xpPerUse` × D, within the gate and the use budget.
5. **The runner judges a boss fight on its rounds to kill** (§5; a lost fight runs on while heroes fall) and reports who deals a boss fight's damage (§4).
6. **The Cellars' fight gold 7–14** (`08` §11's 10–20) **and Vexa's purse gold ×6** (from ×10) — `08` §11's rows need the numbers (its "As tuned" note says so).
7. **The Dread reference party** (runner data): L16, C gear, on Dread, reported until James sets Dread targets.
8. **Systems readings:** auto-equip weighs set bonuses (`03` §6.1 "Auto-equip best"; a hook line of a set bonus is worth `equipment.sets.autoEquipLineScore` = 12 quarter points); each class's default AI rule names the relics that suit it (Warden: the Barrow Bell when an ally is under half, the Grinning Mask against three or more; Blade: the Hourglass Shard in round 1, the Grinning Mask; Ranger: Lantern of the Deep below light 50, Moth Charm; Shade: the Hourglass Shard, Moth Charm; Arcanist: Lantern of the Deep, the Hourglass Shard; Mender: the Brine Pearl on an ally under 35%, the Candle of the Crypt when the party is under 75%, the Tidebound Idol on a Curse); the hero card's Detection counts the loadout passives (`hero.ts` `heroCardStats`, the delve's own number).

### 13.6 Open questions for James (checkpoint 2)

- **Gold for bigger parties:** a four's fight now pays like a trio's, so a delve yields about the same gold for any party (0.8–1.2 R). Keep it, or should bigger parties earn more?
- **Bosses:** their adds now hit half as hard, Vexa and Naesa themselves harder, the Warden and the King themselves softer (a third and a half less), and the fights last 10–13 rounds for the parties meant to meet them. Does a longer boss fight feel better on the phone, and is the Warden's arena (a boss who barely strikes, his Footmen and Hound doing the damage) the fight he should be?
- **Pairs in boss arenas** face a trio's add packs (2 × 0.8 rounds to 2): the pair's boss HP is lower (0.7) to make up for it. Keep that, or count boss adds for a pair downward?
- **Dread:** what should the Dread reference party (L16, C gear: the Cellars 95%, the Crypts 20%, the Vault 0–8%) clear — or should the reference be stronger, or Dread gentler?
- **The Vault's elite fights for small parties** run 12–16 rounds (the champions' Defense): soften them, or accept?
- **07 Q22 — the Hollow King's HP:** ×9 now (×5 in round 2, ×30 in the docs before Phase 7).
